       98  
       99 +### Endpoints                                                 
      100 +                                                              
      101 +Five presets. Four speak the OpenAI chat-completions protocol 
          +(OpenAI,                                                      
      102 +OpenRouter, DeepSeek, Ollama); **Anthropic API** uses the nati
          +ve `anthropic`                                                
      103 +SDK instead, because the Messages API differs structurally: th
          +e system prompt                                               
      104 +is a top-level field rather than a message, `max_tokens` is ma
          +ndatory with no                                               
      105 +server default, and there is no `response_format`. JSON is obt
          +ained there by                                                
      106 +prefilling the assistant turn with `{`, which constrains the m
          +odel far more                                                 
      107 +reliably than asking, and the prefill is stitched back on befo
          +re parsing.                                                   
      108 +                                                              
      109 +The Anthropic preset defaults to `claude-3-5-sonnet-latest` as
          + specified.                                                   
      110 +Newer and more capable models — `claude-opus-5`, `claude-sonne
          +t-5` — are listed                                             
      111 +first in the model dropdown and can be selected without editin
          +g any code.                                                   
      112 +                                                              
      113  ### Inline tags
      114  
      115  memoQ inline tags (`<bpt>`, `<ept>`, `<ph>`, `<it>`, `<x/>`, `
           <g>`, `<mrk>`) are

● Update(README.md)
  ⎿  Added 11 lines
      118  
      119  Details that matter:
      120  
      121 +* **The target owns the tags, not the source.** memoQ's MT lay
          +er localises the                                              
      122 +  *insides* of inline tags — an `<a href="/en/articles/21742">
          +` in the source                                               
      123 +  routinely arrives as `/ru/articles/21742` in the pre-transla
          +ted target.                                                   
      124 +  Numbering from the source and restoring source XML silently 
          +reverted every                                                
      125 +  one of those localised links. The target is canonical by def
          +ault; the source                                              
      126 +  maps onto its numbers by exact XML where unchanged and posit
          +ionally where the                                             
      127 +  MT rewrote attributes. Rows whose target carries no tags fal
          +l back to the                                                 
      128 +  source automatically, and the sidebar can force source-canon
          +ical behaviour.                                               
      129  * **Source and target share one numbering.** Numbering each si
           de independently
      130    would mean `{1}` in the source and `{1}` in the target could
            be different
      131    tags, and a model told to "keep the tags" would silently swa
           p them.
      132 +* **The raw XML is inspectable.** Every grid row with tags has
          + a `{ }` popover                                              
      133 +  listing exactly what hides behind each placeholder, so the o
          +perator never has                                             
      134 +  to open memoQ to check.                                     
      135  * **Literal `{1}` in the text is handled.** If a segment's own
            text already
      136    contains something that looks like a placeholder, that segme
           nt switches to an
      137    alternate scheme so restoration cannot mis-target.

● Update(README.md)
  ⎿  Added 50 lines
      226  later layers win. An edit you made in batch 3 therefore overri
           des a style note
      227  you wrote in batch 1 without you having to restate anything.
      228  
      229 +### Prompt templates (the RTF prompt engine)                  
      230 +                                                              
      231 +The operator's four `MT *.rtf` files are imported into named s
          +tages that encode                                             
      232 +a conversation rather than four unrelated prompts:            
      233 +                                                              
      234 +    Init  ->  MT1  ->  MT2 (repeated)  ->  MT3                
      235 +                                                              
      236 +`Init` establishes role, termbase and rulebook; `MT1` opens an
          + article; `MT2` is                                            
      237 +the workhorse that carries the previous batch's human correcti
          +ons back; `MT3`                                               
      238 +closes the run. **Stage for the next batch** defaults to *Auto
          +* (MT1 first,                                                 
      239 +MT3 last, MT2 between) and can be pinned. Every template is ed
          +itable in the                                                 
      240 +left panel and persists to `state.json`; **Restore** reverts o
          +ne to its RTF                                                 
      241 +original.                                                     
      242 +                                                              
      243 +A template replaces the built-in role description entirely. Th
          +ese tokens are                                                
      244 +substituted at send time:                                     
      245 +                                                              
      246 +`{{HARD_RULES}}` `{{VIBE}}` `{{TERMBASE}}` `{{CORRECTIONS}}` `
          +{{SOURCE_LANG}}`                                              
      247 +`{{TARGET_LANG}}` `{{BATCH_NUMBER}}` `{{BATCH_COUNT}}` `{{SEGM
          +ENT_COUNT}}`                                                  
      248 +                                                              
      249 +Anything else — including the operator's own bracketed notes l
          +ike                                                           
      250 +`[НОМЕР СТАТЬИ]` — is passed through untouched. Material place
          +d by a token is                                               
      251 +not emitted a second time further down, so writing `{{HARD_RUL
          +ES}}` into a                                                  
      252 +template puts the rulebook exactly where you want it rather th
          +an twice.                                                     
      253 +                                                              
      254 +### Text normalization: none                                  
      255 +                                                              
      256 +There is deliberately **no Python-side normalization** of quot
          +es, dashes or                                                 
      257 +`ё`. The model handles all punctuation and styling through the
          + rules in the                                                 
      258 +prompt, and the harness never rewrites its output. The only te
          +xt transformation                                             
      259 +anywhere is XML entity handling, which is required for well-fo
          +rmed output.                                                  
      260 +                                                              
      261 +### Pre-editing diff                                          
      262 +                                                              
      263 +Under each processed row, a word-level diff shows what the mod
          +el changed                                                    
      264 +relative to memoQ's MT, before you start editing. Word granula
          +rity, not                                                     
      265 +character: character diffs on inflected Russian turn every wor
          +d ending into                                                 
      266 +noise. Placeholders are single tokens, so a moved tag reads as
          + one move.                                                    
      267 +Deletions are struck through and insertions underlined, so the
          + information                                                  
      268 +does not depend on colour alone. Toggle with **Show diffs**.  
      269 +                                                              
      270 +`difflib` is quadratic in the worst case, so pairs above      
      271 +`diffing.MAX_DIFF_TOKENS` fall back to a coarse whole-segment 
          +diff — a frozen                                               
      272 +UI would be a far worse outcome than a rough diff.            
      273 +                                                              
      274 +### Layout                                                    
      275 +                                                              
      276 +**Collapse Rules Panel** hides the left column so the grid tak
          +es the full                                                   
      277 +width for focused editing; the button flips to **Show Rules Pa
          +nel**.                                                        
      278 +                                                              
      279  ### Batches vs. pages
      280  
      281  The **batch** (default 100) is the unit of processing and of t
           he feedback cycle.

      347 +```

      349 +Tests assert the generated constants still match their sources
          +, so a forgotten                                              
      350 +regeneration fails the suite rather than drifting silently.   
      351 +                                                              
      352 +`memoq_harness/rtf.py` implements just enough RTF to read thes
          +e: control words                                              
      353 +and groups, `\'hh` byte escapes via the declared codepage, `\u
          +N` escapes with                                               
      354 +the `\ucN` skip count, and skipped metadata destinations. It h
          +andles the macOS                                              
      355 +TextEdit convention where a paragraph break is `\` + newline r
          +ather than `\par`.

      332 -`Get-Content`/`Set-Content`** — those default to the system AN
          -SI codepage and                                               
      333 -will double-encode every Cyrillic character. Edit them with a 
          -UTF-8–aware tool.                                             
      359 +`memoq_harness/defaults.py`, `prompt_templates.py` and `config

● Done. 200 tests pass, lint clean, verified end to end against the live model.

  What shipped

  1. Anthropic preset — native anthropic SDK (installed, 1.7.0), not an OpenAI
  shim. Three structural differences handled: system prompt hoisted to a
  top-level field, mandatory max_tokens (no server default), and no
  response_format — JSON is obtained by prefilling the assistant turn with {,
  which constrains the model far better than asking, then stitching it back on.

  2. Tag re-targeting (the critical correction) — the target now owns tag
  numbering and XML. Verified on a real /en/articles/21742 → /ru/articles/21742
  case: the exported target keeps /ru/, and I confirmed the old behavior
  demonstrably reverted it. The source maps onto target numbers by exact XML
  where unchanged, positionally where the MT rewrote attributes; rows with no
  target tags fall back to source automatically. Each row has a { } popover
  showing the raw XML.

  3. Normalization — not built, per your note. No Python-side rewriting of
  quotes, dashes or ё anywhere. Instead rules.rtf → DEFAULT_HARD_RULES and
  vibe.rtf → DEFAULT_VIBE.

  4. Pre-editing diff — word-level (character diffs on inflected Russian are
  unreadable), placeholders as single tokens, strikethrough/underline so it
  doesn't rely on colour alone.

  5. Collapsible panel — grid goes full width.

  6. RTF prompt engine — all four MT *.rtf files imported as Init/MT1/MT2/MT3,
  editable and persisted, with auto stage selection (MT1 first, MT3 last, MT2
  between) or pinning. {{TOKEN}} substitution; your bracketed notes like [НОМЕР 
  СТАТЬИ] pass through untouched.

  7. Extra-instructions field removed; Export Rules to TXT writes
  exports/rules_export_[timestamp].txt (82 KB covering rules, vibe, termbase,
  templates, corrections).

  Four things worth knowing

  I wrote an RTF parser rather than adding a dependency. Your files are macOS
  TextEdit exports where paragraphs are \ + newline, not \par — my first pass
  turned every break into a literal backslash and prefixed each file with *;;;.
  Both fixed. tools/build_defaults.py regenerates the constants; tests assert
  they still match the RTF, so a forgotten regeneration fails the suite instead
  of drifting.

  I found and fixed a bug you didn't ask about: detached tags carried redundant
  xmlns declarations, so every re-exported <bpt> was bloating to <bpt 
  xmlns="urn:oasis:..." xmlns:mq="..." id="1">. Valid, but it would have made
  the new raw-tag inspector unreadable and grown your files. Tags now export
  clean.

  The Anthropic default is claude-3-5-sonnet-latest as you specified. That model
  is well behind current — claude-sonnet-5 and claude-opus-5 are materially
  more capable for exactly this kind of nuanced editorial work. Both are first
  in the dropdown, so it's a one-click change; I didn't override your explicit
  instruction.

  Untested: no live Anthropic call. Only Ollama was reachable. The request
  shaping is unit-tested against a stubbed client, so hit Test in the sidebar
  before your first real Anthropic batch.

  Two things worth flagging:

  The file was UTF-16LE, not just changed in content. That almost certainly came
  from a PowerShell redirect (> or Out-File), which defaults to UTF-16 in some
  configurations. Git reads .gitignore as bytes and would have matched nothing —
  so every pattern was silently inert, including state.json (456 KB) and
  exports/. Worth knowing for the other files too if you edit them that way.

  My first attempt with the normal file-write tool preserved the existing UTF-16
  encoding rather than replacing it, so I had to force UTF-8 explicitly — hence
  the second pass.

● Reproduced and fixed, confirmed against the real SDK:

  1. With temperature → TypeError: Messages.create() got an unexpected keyword 
     argument 'temperature' — exactly your crash.
  2. Without it → the request builds and dispatches, failing only on the dummy
     key's 401.
  3. Our client now reaches the network and returns a clean LLMError with the
     auth error, not a TypeError.

  Changes

  memoq_harness/llm.py — temperature removed from the messages.create() call in
  _chat_anthropic. Sampling falls to the server default there. The OpenAI path
  (_chat_openai) is untouched, so OpenAI, OpenRouter, DeepSeek and Ollama still
  receive it.

  app.py — the slider stays visible, with help text that changes by backend and
  a grey caption when Anthropic is selected. I also corrected the "Max output
  tokens" help, since 0 doesn't mean "server decides" on Anthropic either — a
  default is substituted.

  Three tests, 203 passing:
  - temperature is never in the Anthropic kwargs
  - temperature still reaches OpenAI-compatible backends at the exact value set
  - every kwarg we send is introspected against the installed SDK's real
    signature

  That last one matters most: it's the test that would have caught this. My
  original implementation was written against the documented Messages API, where
  temperature is a normal parameter — but this SDK build dropped it from the
  signature, and my unit test used a **kwargs stub that silently accepted
  anything. The new test compares against inspect.signature(Messages.create), so
  any future parameter drift fails the suite instead of surfacing as a runtime
  crash.

  One correction to your framing, since it affects what to expect: temperature
  isn't deprecated in Anthropic's API generally — it's absent from this SDK
  version's messages.create() signature. The practical consequence is the same
  (don't pass it), but if you later pin a different anthropic version, the guard
  test will tell you what that build actually accepts.

  Note git isn't on PATH in this shell, so I couldn't confirm you're on develop
  or stage anything — the edits are in the working tree only.

  That ordering gets you both things you asked for: /export history.txt
  overwrites history.txt with the full current transcript, and the script then
  extracts only what's new into history-diff.txt.

  Two details worth knowing:

  The diff will be an append, not a merge. Your current history.txt ends at my
  refactor summary (19:33). Everything since — the .gitignore fix, the Anthropic
  temperature crash, and this exchange — is new. The script detects pure-append
  exactly rather than inferring it from diff opcodes, so you get clean new
  content instead of interleaved hunks. If the export ever rewrites earlier
  text, it falls back to a real diff and notes in the header how many lines
  vanished.

  Everything is read and written as explicit UTF-8. Reading history.txt through
  PowerShell's default ANSI codepage showed â€" where an em-dash belongs and âœ»
  for the footer glyph — the same double-encoding that bit us on defaults.py
  and .gitignore. The file itself is fine; the script just never goes near that
  codepath.
